    "originalFile": "\\vspace{-1mm}\n\\section{Discussion}\n\\label{sec:discussion}\n\n\\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}\n\\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($4.97\\times$ Mistral, $3.93\\times$ Qwen, $2.88\\times$ LLaMA) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. The finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.\n\n\\paragraph{Dual-variable mechanism.}\n\\looseness=-1 Cross-model extraction gain requires two necessary conditions: diverse traces (low $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$) and substantial self-bias (high BR). Among five frontier models under cross-model extraction (Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), R1-Distill-8B satisfies both ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}} = 0.248$, $\\text{BR} = 2.92$) and gains $+2.45$\\,pp; o3 has moderate diversity ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}} = 0.596$) but near-minimal bias ($\\text{BR} = 1.66$), yielding $\\Delta = 0$. Neither condition alone suffices.\n\n\\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}\n\\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",

135	
136	\paragraph{Method-level interventions.} ICG ($\Delta \approx 0$, $p = .206$), Contrastive ($\Delta = -9.31$\,pp, $p < 0.01$), and Debate ($\Delta = -18.63$/$-23.00$\,pp, $p < 0.001$) all fail (Table~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, Appendix~\ref{app:remediation}). Ten further experiments (code verification, iterative refinement, logprob filtering, cross-model ensembles) produce neutral-to-negative results (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:additional_interventions}).
137	
138	\paragraph{Independence-accuracy trade-off.} Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization} compares Fleiss' $\kappa$ across four aggregation methods and three model families (Figure~\ref{fig:kappa-accuracy}). SC ($\kappa = 0.44$), Diverse-Prompt ($\kappa = 0.40$), and ICG ($\kappa = 0.47$) cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp. All three families cluster above $\kappa = 0.40$, forming a same-model trade-off ceiling at the 7B--32B scale tested: methods that increase independence destroy accuracy, and methods preserving accuracy cannot break the correlation floor. The bias ratio gradient ($4.97\times$ Mistral $> 3.93\times$ Qwen $> 2.88\times$ LLaMA) suggests weaker models suffer more severe confirmation bias; all three exceed $1.0$, confirming universality across families. For practitioners, $C_0$ combined with SC accuracy predicts when augmentation is unlikely to help: $C_0 < 5\%$ implies degeneration, SC $\geq 60\%$ implies confirmation bias, SC $\leq 40\%$ implies error amplification. Whether 70B+ models or trained extractors shift the observed trade-off boundary remains open.
139	
140	\begin{table}[t]
141	\centering
142	\small

_source line 19496 · 2026-05-21T20:33:25.094Z_

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_011tvqd2e3KTqe9QuZq6GTUK`</summary>

```text
0	\paragraph{Scale and language.}
1	Open-source models range from 7B to 32B parameters; frontier extension (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) confirms SCB universality (BR $1.66$--$4.97\times$) but uses cross-model extraction via Mistral-7B rather than self-extraction, as closed-source APIs do not expose internal traces. All datasets are English-language logical reasoning benchmarks with discrete answer spaces. We do not evaluate trained symbolic extractors, external solvers with verified grounding, or multilingual settings. Oracle analysis is limited to FOLIO and ProofWriter (gold annotations available); extending to commonsense reasoning requires constructing comparable gold constraint sets. The cross-architecture NLI verifier (\S\ref{sec:cross-arch}) provides no benefit on LogiQA (combo $\Delta \leq +1$\,pp, all $p > 0.5$), indicating that cross-architecture escape requires task-verifier alignment---NLI is effective only when the task naturally decomposes into premise-conclusion entailment. Even on compatible tasks, NLI gain vanishes when the capability gap is large (Qwen3-14B ProofWriter: SC 85.83\% vs.\ DeBERTa 55\%, gap $= -30.83$\,pp, $\Delta \leq +0.50$\,pp, n.s.), and moderate negative gaps yield small, non-significant gains (LLaMA-3.1-8B: gap $= -17.50$\,pp, $+0.83$\,pp, n.s.; \S\ref{sec:cross-arch}). Scaling the cross-architecture approach---via trained verifiers, code-based symbolic execution, or multi-verifier ensembles---remains unexplored and may extend the escape to stronger generators and broader task types.
2	
3	\paragraph{Capability-gap analysis.}
4	The capability-gap analysis rests on $n{=}6$ verifier--generator conditions, of which four share the same DeBERTa verifier. While each condition pairs the verifier with a different generator (producing gains ranging from $+0.5$\,pp to $+6.67$\,pp), the shared verifier component may inflate apparent consistency. A verifier-collapsed analysis with only three independent verifiers (DeBERTa-pooled, RoBERTa, BART) retains the monotonic ordering but at $p = 0.167$, below conventional significance. We therefore characterize the capability-gap relationship as a consistent empirical pattern rather than a statistically confirmed law.
```

</details>

All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp).\n",
    "structuredPatch": [

        "oldLines": 7,
        "newStart": 3,
        "newLines": 7,
        "lines": [
          " \\label{sec:discussion}",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}",
          "-\\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models from 7B to frontier; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. The finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent across three families. Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.",
          "+\\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($1.66$--$4.97\\times$ across nine models from 7B to frontier; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \\citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. The finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent from 7B open-source to frontier closed-source. Cross-model extraction gain requires two conditions: diverse traces (low $\\kappa_{\\text{proc}}$) \\emph{and} substantial self-bias (high BR); neither alone suffices (Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}). Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.",
          " ",
          " \\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}",
          " \\looseness=-1 $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \\citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\\kappa \\geq 0.40$ \\citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\\,pp, $+14.22$\\,pp)."

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_017WVFgLLKDHeiagtsiZVj5F`</summary>

44	
45	\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\times$ (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}).
46	
47	\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes across scale: $4.97\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). SCB persists from 7B open-source to frontier closed-source models: BR ranges from $1.66\times$ (o3) to $4.97\times$ across nine models (Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.
48	
49	\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. First, BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors (SC $\geq 10/12$) amplify to $\text{BR} = 18.8\times$ versus $2.7\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.
50	
51	\subsection{Process-Level Independence}
52	\label{sec:process-kappa}
53	
54	We define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:
55	\begin{equation}
56	\label{eq:process-sim}
57	\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \sum_{i < j} \cos(\mathbf{v}_i^q, \mathbf{v}_j^q),
58	\end{equation}
59	where $\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks. Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim (Spearman $\rho = -1.0$, $n = 5$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\ref{tab:process-gradient}), but gain also requires substantial self-bias---o3 ($\text{BR} = 1.66$) gains nothing despite moderate diversity ($\kappa_{\text{proc}} = 0.60$).
60	
61	
62	\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}
63	\label{sec:cil}
```

    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes across scale: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). SCB persists from 7B open-source to frontier closed-source models: BR ranges from $1.66\\times$ (o3) to $4.97\\times$ across nine models (Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. First, BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks. Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim (Spearman $\\rho = -1.0$, $n = 5$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), but gain also requires substantial self-bias---o3 ($\\text{BR} = 1.66$) gains nothing despite moderate diversity ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}} = 0.60$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_main}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral $\\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen-14B $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nQwen-14B $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",

Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\nwhere $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks. Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim (Spearman $\\rho = -1.0$, $n = 5$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), but gain also requires substantial self-bias---o3 ($\\text{BR} = 1.66$) gains nothing despite moderate diversity ($\\kappa_{\\text{proc}} = 0.60$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_main}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral $\\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen-14B $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nQwen-14B $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",

<details><summary>↩ Tool result · `toolu_01YF4PQnrAumJxRFdg4XhwSV`</summary>

```text
1	\vspace{-1mm}
2	\section{Discussion}
3	\label{sec:discussion}
4	
5	\paragraph{Self-verification and BR magnitude.}
6	\looseness=-1 Our bias ratio ($1.66$--$4.97\times$ across nine models from 7B to frontier; Table~\ref{tab:frontier-scb}) quantifies why self-correction fails without external feedback \citep{huang2024selfcorrect, stechly2025selfverification, chen2025sets}: extraction and generation share distributional biases. The finding is the magnitude---nearly 5:1 on incorrect problems, consistent from 7B open-source to frontier closed-source. Cross-model extraction gain requires two conditions: diverse traces (low $\kappa_{\text{proc}}$) \emph{and} substantial self-bias (high BR); neither alone suffices (Table~\ref{tab:process-gradient}). Any self-verification pipeline deriving criteria from the same model faces this bottleneck, whether formal constraints, natural-language critiques, or code assertions.
7	
8	\paragraph{Test-time scaling and practitioner implications.}
9	\looseness=-1 $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20$--$3.62$ for nominal $K = 12$ means sampling beyond a dozen traces yields diminishing independent information; the pattern intensifies with capability (Qwen3 thinking: $\kappa = 0.91$, $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.09$), creating a scaling paradox for o1/R1-style models \citep{guo2025deepseekr1}. All non-destructive interventions cluster at $\kappa \geq 0.40$ \citep{krogh1994neural}; Debate reaches $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy ($-18.6$\,pp). Cross-architecture escape succeeds ($p = 0.003$) because encoder-only and decoder-only models have structurally different error profiles. Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\leq 40\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\,pp). For intermediate-SC models, gain tracks the capability gap monotonically ($p = 0.003$); the bottleneck is extraction quality, as oracle constraints succeed ($+29.90$\,pp, $+14.22$\,pp).
10	
```

Two diagnostics predict failure: $C_0 < 5\\%$ means SICA reduces to SC; SC $\\leq 40\\%$ risks error amplification ($-2.83$\\,pp).     "originalFile": "\\section{Introduction}\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nTest-time computation scaling through multiple reasoning samples has emerged as a practical alternative to scaling model answer.\nSC is simple, model-agnostic, and effective---but it is hitting a ceiling.\n\\citet{loo2025reevaluating} demonstrate that gains from increasing $K$ plateau and even decline for strong models.\nThe gap between SC's theoretical promise (independent correct voters converge to truth) and its practical behavior (saturating gains) points to a structural mismatch: same-model traces carry correlated errors, and majority voting has no mechanism to correct shared bias.\n\nConfidence-weighted \\citep{taubenfeld2025cisc} and universal \\citep{chen2024usc} extensions alleviate this, but the core limitation persists.\nRecent neuro-symbolic methods \\citep{pan2023logiclm, olausson2023linc, xu2026dylos, li2025aristotle, wang2025fover, luo2025logicagent} report gains on logical reasoning but introduce components beyond the reasoning model.\nA simpler question precedes these: can the \\emph{same} model extract useful formal constraints from its own traces---a zero-cost upgrade requiring no additional model or data?\nNo prior work has systematically evaluated this.\n\nWe ground our analysis in the Condorcet Jury Theorem (CJT) \\citep{condorcet1785}: if $K$ independent voters each answer correctly with probability $p > 0.5$, the majority converges to truth \\citep{austensmith1996}.\nWhen independence fails \\citep{ladha1992correlated}, additional samples carry diminishing information.\nMeasuring inter-trace agreement with Fleiss' $\\kappa$ \\citep{fleiss1971kappa}, we find $\\kappa = 0.21$--$0.82$ across instruction-tuned conditions, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}}$ that The $\\pm 2$\\,pp margin corresponds to the lower bound of gains reported in the SC literature \\citep{wang2023sc} (2--5\\,pp); $\\pm 1$\\,pp yields 0 equivalent conditions (0/2/14), $\\pm 3$\\,pp yields 10 (10/0/6). This absolute margin is more conservative at high baselines ($\\pm 2.4\\%$ relative at 85\\%) than low ones ($\\pm 5.1\\%$ at 39\\%), working against our equivalence claim where SICA most plausibly helps (relative $\\pm 5\\%$ margin yields 8 equivalent; Appendix~\\ref{app:relative_tost}). We trace the failure below.\n\n\n\\subsection{Oracle Control}\n\\label{sec:oracle}\n\nReplacing self-extracted constraints with gold annotations (FOLIO logical formulae, ProofWriter DSL), Oracle-SICA yields $+29.90$\\,pp on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($p < 10^{-13}$), $+14.22$\\,pp on Qwen2.5-14B FOLIO ($p = 2.0 \\times 10^{-7}$), and $+17.17$\\,pp on ProofWriter ($p < 10^{-14}$). Z3 agrees with gold labels on 95.1\\% (FOLIO) and 97.1\\% (ProofWriter). The MAX-SAT mechanism is sound; Mistral-7B's best self-extracted $\\Delta$ ($+3.43$\\,pp) captures only 11.5\\% of its oracle ceiling. For Qwen2.5-14B on ProofWriter, the gap is wider still: self-extraction degrades accuracy ($-2.50$\\,pp) against an oracle ceiling of $+17.17$\\,pp, indicating that stronger models do not self-extract more effectively.\n\n\n",

          " We evaluate SICA against Self-Consistency (SC) across six models, three reasoning domains, and over 3,000 condition-evaluations (${\\sim}$1,250 unique instances), with diagnostic analyses of confirmation bias and process-level independence (\\S\\ref{sec:analysis}).",
          " ",
          "-\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B parameters). Frontier model extension (gpt-4o, gpt-4.1, gpt-5.5, o3, Gemini-2.5-Pro) evaluates SCB universality (Appendix~\\ref{app:frontier-scb}).",
          "+\\paragraph{Models.} Qwen2.5-14B-Instruct, Qwen2.5-7B-Instruct, Qwen3-14B (non-thinking mode)~\\citep{yang2024qwen25}, Mistral-7B-Instruct-v0.3~\\citep{jiang2023mistral}, LLaMA-3.1-8B-Instruct~\\citep{dubey2024llama3}, and QwQ-32B~\\citep{qwen2024qwq} (7B--32B). StrategyQA~\\citep{geva2021strategyqa} ($n{=}200$) serves as a domain-formalizability diagnostic. GSM8K~\\citep{cobbe2021gsm8k} ($n{=}30$) and AIME 2024 ($n{=}22$) probe the degenerate regime ($C_0 < 5\\%$).",

    "content": "- update-config: Use this skill to configure the Claude Code harness via settings.json. Automated behaviors (\"from now on when X\", \"each time X\", \"whenever X\", \"before/after X\") require hooks configured in settings.json - the harness executes these, not Claude, so memory/preferences cannot fulfill them. Also use for: permissions (\"allow X\", \"add permission\", \"move permission to\"), env vars (\"set X=Y\"), hook troubleshooting, or any changes to settings.json/settings.local.json files. Examples: \"allow npm commands\", \"add bq permission to global settings\", \"move permission to user settings\", \"set DEBUG=true\", \"when claude stops show X\". For simple settings like theme/model, suggest the /config command.\n- keybindings-help: Use when the user wants to customize keyboard shortcuts, rebind keys, add chord bindings, or modify ~/.claude/keybindings.json. Examples: \"rebind ctrl+s\", \"add a chord shortcut\", \"change the submit key\", \"customize keybindings\".\n- simplify: Review changed code for reuse, quality, and efficiency, then fix any issues found.\n- fewer-permission-prompts: Scan your transcripts for common read-only Bash and MCP tool calls, then add a prioritized allowlist to project .claude/settings.json to reduce permission prompts.\n- loop: Run a prompt or slash command on a recurring interval (e.g. /loop 5m /foo). Omit the interval to let the model self-pace. - When the user wants to set up a recurring task, poll for status, or run something repeatedly on an interval (e.g. \"check the deploy every 5 minutes\", \"keep running /babysit-prs\"). Do NOT invoke for one-off tasks.\n- schedule: Create, update, list, or run scheduled remote agents (routines) that execute on a cron schedule. - When the user wants to schedule a recurring remote agent, set up automated tasks, create a cron job for Claude Code, or manage their scheduled agents/routines. Also use when the user wants a one-time scheduled run (\"run this once at 3pm\", \"remind me to check X tomorrow\").\n- claude-api: Build, debug, and optimize Claude API / Anthropic SDK apps. Apps built with this skill should include prompt caching. Also handles migrating existing Claude API code between Claude model versions (4.5 → 4.6, 4.6 → 4.7, retired-model replacements).\nTRIGGER when: code imports `anthropic`/`@anthropic-ai/sdk`; user asks for the Claude API, Anthropic SDK, or Managed Agents; user adds/modifies/tunes a Claude feature (caching, thinking, compaction, tool use, batch, files, citations, memory) or model (Opus/Sonnet/Haiku) in a file; questions about prompt caching / cache hit rate in an Anthropic SDK project.\nSKIP: file imports `openai`/other-provider SDK, filename like `*-openai.py`/`*-generic.py`, provider-neutral code, general programming/ML.\n- init: Initialize a new CLAUDE.md file with codebase documentation\n- review: Review a pull request\n- security-review: Complete a security review of the pending changes on the current branch",

60	
61	
62	\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}
63	\label{sec:cil}
64	
65	\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):
66	\begin{equation}
67	\label{eq:cil-main}
68	\text{CIL}(V, G) = \log_2 \frac{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ wrong})}{P(V \text{ correct} \mid G \text{ correct})}.
69	\end{equation}
70	$\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\text{CIL}(\text{self}, G) \ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.
71	
72	\begin{table}[t]
73	\centering
74	\small
75	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
76	\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}
77	\label{tab:cil_main}
78	\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}
79	\toprule
80	Generator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\Delta$ (pp) \\
81	\midrule
82	Mistral $\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\
83	Mistral $\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\
84	Mistral $\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\
85	\addlinespace
86	Qwen-14B $\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\
87	Mistral $\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\
88	\addlinespace
89	Mistral $\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\
90	Qwen-14B $\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\
91	Mistral $\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\
92	\bottomrule
93	\end{tabular}
94	\end{table}
95	
96	\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}
97	\label{sec:diagnostics}
98	
99	Further diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\ref{app:extraction}--\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\sim}$10\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\text{BR} = 4.97\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.
100	
101	
102	\subsection{Remediation Analysis}
103	\label{sec:remediation}
104	\label{sec:interventions}
105	
106	\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \ref{tab:remediation}, \ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\kappa \approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.
107	
108	\begin{figure*}[t]
109	\centering
110	\includegraphics[width=0.85\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}
111	\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\pm 1$\,pp noise zone.}
112	\label{fig:interventions-summary}
113	\end{figure*}

0	\section{Analysis}
1	\label{sec:analysis}
2	
3	We test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\S\ref{sec:mirage}).
4	
5	\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}
6	\label{sec:effective-k}
7	
8	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\kappa \in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
9

12	\small

48	\label{sec:confirmation-bias}
49	\label{sec:effective-k}
50	
51	Table~\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\text{eff}} = 1.20\text{--}3.62$. Two conditions are jointly necessary for any positive trend: sufficient $K_{\text{eff}}$ ($\leq 1.30$ never improves) and sufficient SC accuracy ($K_{\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\%$ produces significant degradation). Cross-model validation confirms universality: all four families produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$ (Table~\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).
52	
53	This correlation manifests through a specific mechanism: self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer regardless of correctness. On Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$), when SICA errs, 83.3\% of extracted constraints support the incorrect majority answer---a bias ratio of $4.97\times$ compared to correct problems. Consequently, 94\% of SICA errors overlap with SC errors: symbolic aggregation adds no independent corrective signal.
54	
55	The causal chain is: the model generates an answer, then extracts logical constraints consistent with it. Constraints from majority-answer traces use trace-specific variable names (56.4\% of cross-answer pairs share zero Z3 variables), so MAX-SAT retains nearly all, and aggregation reduces to a weighted majority vote (Eq.~\ref{eq:degenerate}). Confirmation bias generalizes across model families: bias ratios of $4.97\times$ (Mistral-7B), $3.93\times$ (Qwen2.5-14B), and $2.88{\times}^{\dag}$ (LLaMA-3.1-8B) all significantly exceed $1.0$ ($^{\dag}$based on 4/12 traces due to extraction failures; Table~\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}). The oracle ceiling (\S\ref{sec:oracle}) confirms that 75.9\% of the potential gain is lost to self-extraction quality.
56

```text
1	\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}
2	\label{sec:solutions}
3	\label{sec:cross-arch}
4	
5	\paragraph{Method.}
6	We test with DeBERTa-large \citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.
7	
8	\paragraph{Main result.}
9	\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\%) and 55.00\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\S\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\ $p = 6.78 \times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\ $p = 7.41 \times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.
10	
11	\paragraph{Why it works.}
12	Three conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\S\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \textsc{False} (71.43\% vs.\ 38.10\%), SC dominates \textsc{Unknown} (68.12\% vs.\ 18.84\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \geq 3$ (Appendix~\ref{app:multi-nli}).
13	
14	\enlargethispage{2\baselineskip}
15	\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}
16	\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\% standalone; Table~\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.
17	
18	\begin{table}[t]
19	\centering
20	\small
21	\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}
22	\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}
23	\resizebox{\columnwidth}{!}{\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
24	\toprule
25	Generator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\
26	\midrule
27	Mistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\,pp & $<$0.001 \\
28	LLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\,pp & 0.66 \\
29	Qwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\,pp & 0.50 \\
30	Qwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\,pp & 0.45 \\
31	\bottomrule
32	\end{tabular}}
33	\end{table}
34	
35	\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}
36	\label{sec:drc}
37	
38	Full SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\tau$; above $\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.
39	
40	\begin{table}[t]
41	\centering
42	\small
43	\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\% NLI cost across both datasets.}
44	\label{tab:drc}
45	\setlength{\tabcolsep}{3pt}
46	\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}
47	\toprule
48	Dataset & Method & Acc.\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\
49	\midrule
50	\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\% & 0/600 & 0\% & --- \\

      "content": "\\section{Cross-Architecture Proof-of-Concept}\n\\label{sec:solutions}\n\\label{sec:cross-arch}\n\n\\paragraph{Method.}\nWe test with DeBERTa-large \\citep{he2021deberta} (406M, encoder-only), fine-tuned on MultiNLI \\citep{williams2018mnli}, classifying (premise, conclusion) pairs as entailment, contradiction, or neutral. In combo mode, each NLI-supporting vote counts $w$ times alongside SC's $K$ votes. We evaluate on Mistral-7B FOLIO ($n = 204$) and ProofWriter depth-5 ($n = 600$), whose (premise, conclusion) structure suits NLI classification.\n\n\\paragraph{Main result.}\n\\looseness=-1 DeBERTa NLI alone achieves 49.51\\% on FOLIO (below SC's 55.88\\%) and 55.00\\% on ProofWriter (above SC's 39.33\\%). On FOLIO ($n = 204$), combo mode yields $+0.49$\\,pp at $w{=}1$ and $+2.94$\\,pp at $w{=}3$ (both n.s.). On ProofWriter ($n = 600$), $w{=}3$ achieves $+6.67$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$) and $w{=}5$ reaches $+11.00$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$)---while same-model SICA \\emph{degrades} ProofWriter ($-2.83$\\,pp, raw $p = 0.014$; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$; Table~\\ref{tab:main_results}), illustrating that corrective signal must originate outside the model's distribution (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}). We report $w{=}3$ as primary. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), Mistral-7B $w{=}3$ (adj.\\ $p = 6.78 \\times 10^{-5}$) and $w{=}5$ (adj.\\ $p = 7.41 \\times 10^{-6}$) remain significant; all other conditions are non-significant.\n\n\\paragraph{Why it works.}\nThree conditions jointly enable the escape: (i)~\\emph{structural independence}---encoder-only DeBERTa and decoder-only Mistral share no architecture or training data (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}); (ii)~\\emph{positive capability gap}---verifier standalone accuracy exceeds generator SC (Finding~2); (iii)~\\emph{task-verifier alignment}---NLI classification naturally matches premise-conclusion structure. Per-label complementarity: NLI excels on \\textsc{False} (71.43\\% vs.\\ 38.10\\%), SC dominates \\textsc{Unknown} (68.12\\% vs.\\ 18.84\\%). All three NLI architectures produce significant gains at $w \\geq 3$ (Appendix~\\ref{app:multi-nli}).\n\n\\enlargethispage{2\\baselineskip}\n\\paragraph{Capability gap hypothesis.}\n\\looseness=-1 Four generators spanning a 46-point SC range on ProofWriter ($n{=}600$), all paired with DeBERTa-large-mnli (55.00\\% standalone; Table~\\ref{tab:cross_generator_nli}): only Mistral-7B (SC 39.33\\%, below the verifier) gains significantly ($+6.67$\\,pp, $p < 0.001$); LLaMA-3.1-8B ($+0.83$), Qwen2.5-14B ($+0.67$), and Qwen3-14B ($+0.50$\\,pp) show no improvement. After BH correction ($m{=}12$), only Mistral-7B survives. The monotonic relationship holds across three NLI architectures, spanning six verifier-generator pairs (Spearman $\\rho = 1.0$, $p = 0.003$, $n{=}6$; four of six share the DeBERTa verifier---see Limitations; Figure~\\ref{fig:capability-gap}, Appendix~\\ref{app:capability-gap}), confirming Finding~2.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{Cross-generator NLI escape with DeBERTa-large-mnli verifier (55.00\\% standalone, ProofWriter $n{=}600$). Combo gain decreases monotonically with generator capability ($w{=}3$).}\n\\label{tab:cross_generator_nli}\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator & Family & SC & Gap & Combo $\\Delta$ ($w{=}3$) & $p$ \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral-7B & Mistral & 39.33\\% & $+$15.67 & $+$6.67\\,pp & $<$0.001 \\\\\nLLaMA-3.1-8B & LLaMA & 72.50\\% & $-$17.50 & $+$0.83\\,pp & 0.66 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & Qwen & 74.00\\% & $-$19.00 & $+$0.67\\,pp & 0.50 \\\\\nQwen3-14B & Qwen & 85.83\\% & $-$30.83 & $+$0.50\\,pp & 0.45 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Adaptive Verification via Disagreement Routing}\n\\label{sec:drc}\n\nFull SC+NLI ensemble invokes NLI on every question, but high-agreement questions gain little from external verification. \\textbf{Disagreement-Routed Cascade (DRC)} invokes DeBERTa-large-mnli only when the fraction of $K$ traces voting for the majority answer falls below threshold $\\tau$; above $\\tau$, SC majority vote is used directly.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\caption{DRC on Mistral-7B ($K{=}12$, DeBERTa-large-mnli, $w{=}3$). $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 83--90\\% of full-combo gain at 35--39\\% NLI cost across both datasets.}\n\\label{tab:drc}\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\nDataset & Method & Acc.\\ & NLI Calls & Cost & Recovery \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{PW-D5} & SC only & 39.33\\% & 0/600 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{45.33\\%} & \\textbf{233/600} & \\textbf{38.8\\%} & \\textbf{90.0\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 46.00\\% & 600/600 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multirow{3}{*}{FOLIO} & SC only & 55.88\\% & 0/204 & 0\\% & --- \\\\\n& \\textbf{DRC $\\tau{=}0.7$} & \\textbf{58.33\\%} & \\textbf{71/204} & \\textbf{34.8\\%} & \\textbf{83.3\\%} \\\\\n& Full combo & 58.82\\% & 204/204 & 100\\% & 100\\% \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\looseness=-1 DRC at $\\tau{=}0.7$ recovers 90\\% of full-combo gain on ProofWriter ($+6.00$ out of $+6.67$\\,pp) at 38.8\\% NLI cost, and 83.3\\% on held-out FOLIO ($+2.45$ out of $+2.94$\\,pp) at 34.8\\% cost---confirming that per-question agreement routing generalizes without re-tuning $\\tau$ (selected on ProofWriter; SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}",
    "newString": "\\looseness=-1 Table~\\ref{tab:independence} (Appendix) reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).",
    "originalFile": "\\section{Analysis}\n\\label{sec:analysis}\n\nWe test the three predictions of the confirmation bias hypothesis (\\S\\ref{sec:mirage}).\n\n\\subsection{Condorcet Effective-K}\n\\label{sec:effective-k}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:independence} reports Fleiss' $\\kappa$ and Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}) across 15 conditions at $K = 12$. Among instruction-tuned models, $\\kappa$ ranges from 0.21 to 0.82, yielding $K_{\\text{eff}} = 1.20\\text{--}3.62$. Across four Mistral-7B seeds, $\\kappa \\in [0.41, 0.51]$ yet $\\Delta$ ranges from $+3.43$ to $-0.49$\\,pp, indicating seed-level constraint quality matters beyond independence. Positive $\\Delta$ requires both sufficient $K_{\\text{eff}}$ ($\\leq 1.30$ never improves) and SC accuracy ($K_{\\text{eff}} = 3.62$ with SC $= 39\\%$ degrades). All four model families produce $\\kappa \\geq 0.41$ (Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}).\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Trace independence metrics ($K = 12$, sorted by $\\kappa$ descending). $\\kappa$: Fleiss' Kappa. $K_{\\text{eff}}$: Effective-K (Eq.~\\ref{eq:effk}). SC: Self-Consistency baseline. $\\Delta$: SICA $-$ SC (pp). {*}\\,$p < 0.05$ (uncorrected; BH adj.\\ $p = 0.112$, $m{=}16$); positive $\\Delta$ values do not reach significance. multi-T $= T \\in \\{0.3, 0.7, 1.2\\}$, 4 traces each.}\n\\label{tab:independence}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llccrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nModel & Condition & $\\kappa$ & $K_{\\text{eff}}$ & SC (\\%) & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nQwen3 (think) & FOLIO & 0.91 & 1.09 & ${\\sim}$84 & ${\\sim}$0 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & LogiQA & 0.82 & 1.20 & 78.50 & $-$0.50 \\\\\nQwen3 & FOLIO & 0.77 & 1.26 & 84.80 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nQwen2.5-14B & FOLIO & 0.74 & 1.30 & 75.49 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nQwen2.5-7B & FOLIO & 0.66 & 1.46 & 75.98 & $+$0.49 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.3$) & FOLIO & 0.53 & 1.77 & 58.33 & $-$1.47 \\\\\nMistral-7B & LogiQA & 0.51 & 1.81 & 51.00 & $+$0.50 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s456) & FOLIO & 0.51 & 1.82 & 55.88 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & FOLIO & 0.50 & 1.84 & 55.88 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s123) & FOLIO & 0.49 & 1.88 & 54.41 & $+$2.94 \\\\\nMistral-7B (s789) & FOLIO & 0.41 & 2.17 & 54.41 & $-$0.49 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}0.5$) & FOLIO & 0.52 & 1.80 & 58.33 & $-$0.98 \\\\\nMistral-7B (multi-T) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 57.84 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B ($T{=}1.0$) & FOLIO & 0.48 & 1.91 & 55.39 & 0.00 \\\\\nMistral-7B & PW-D5 & 0.21 & 3.62 & 39.33 & $\\mathbf{-2.83}${*} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\n\\subsection{Symbolic Confirmation Bias}\n\\label{sec:confirmation-bias}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Self-extracted constraints systematically confirm the model's majority answer. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, when SICA errs, 83.3\\% of constraints support the incorrect answer---bias ratio $4.97\\times$ (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias})---and 94\\% of errors overlap with SC. Z3 rejects only 3.4\\% of constraints, confirming degeneration (Eq.~\\ref{eq:degenerate}).\n\n\\looseness=-1 Bias generalizes across scale: $4.97\\times$ [3.98, 6.37] (Mistral, 12-trace), $3.93\\times$ [2.44, 7.11] (Qwen-14B, 12-trace), $2.88\\times$ (LLaMA, 4-trace$^{\\dag}$), all $> 1.0$ (95\\% bootstrap CIs for Mistral/Qwen; Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-bias}; weighted BRs in Appendix~\\ref{app:cross-model-bias}). SCB persists across nine models from 7B to frontier (BR $1.66$--$4.97\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:frontier-scb}). Constraints use trace-specific variables (56.4\\% zero cross-answer overlap), collapsing aggregation to majority voting.\n\n\\looseness=-1 Two controls rule out answer leakage. First, BR is uniform across early ($5.37$), mid ($5.25$), and late ($5.16$) reasoning steps (Fisher $p = 0.71$); high-consensus errors (SC $\\geq 10/12$) amplify to $\\text{BR} = 18.8\\times$ versus $2.7\\times$ for low-consensus ($p < 0.001$). Second, removing the answer sentence from CoT traces before extraction yields no BR decrease across three truncation strategies (Wilcoxon $p \\geq 0.12$, smallest $p = 0.12$, conservative truncation; per-problem mean BR: full $2.79$, truncated $2.26$--$2.66$, computed over problems with finite ratio) despite significant accuracy loss (McNemar $p < 0.013$). SCB is consistent with a distributional prior embedded throughout reasoning, not answer contamination.\n\n\\subsection{Process-Level Independence}\n\\label{sec:process-kappa}\n\nWe define process similarity as the mean pairwise cosine similarity of TF-IDF trace representations:\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:process-sim}\n\\text{ProcessSim}(q) = \\frac{2}{K(K{-}1)} \\sum_{i < j} \\cos(\\mathbf{v}_i^q, \\mathbf{v}_j^q),\n\\end{equation}\n\\looseness=-1 where $\\mathbf{v}_i^q$ is the TF-IDF vector of trace $t_i$ for question $q$. On Mistral-7B FOLIO, mean ProcessSim $= 0.69$, far exceeding answer-level $\\kappa = 0.46$: traces share reasoning processes even when answers diverge. The gap generalizes across four families (Table~\\ref{tab:cross-model-process}), ranging from $0.31$ (LLaMA) to $0.02$ (Qwen-14B), revealing hidden process homogeneity that answer diversity masks. Under cross-model extraction, SICA gain decreases monotonically with ProcessSim ($\\rho = -1.0$, $p = 0.008$; Table~\\ref{tab:process-gradient}), modulated by self-bias severity.\n\n\n\\subsection{Cumulative Information Lift}\n\\label{sec:cil}\n\n\\looseness=-1 We unify the preceding diagnostics under \\textit{Cumulative Information Lift} (CIL):\n\\begin{equation}\n\\label{eq:cil-main}\n\\text{CIL}(V, G) = \\log_2 \\frac{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ wrong})}{P(V \\text{ correct} \\mid G \\text{ correct})}.\n\\end{equation}\n$\\text{CIL} > 0$ when verifier $V$ preferentially corrects generator $G$'s errors; $\\text{CIL} < 0$ indicates shared bias. Table~\\ref{tab:cil_main} reports CIL for eight verifier--generator pairs. All cross-architecture NLI pairs on ProofWriter yield $\\text{CIL} > 0$, while same-model SICA yields $\\text{CIL} < 0$. CIL strongly predicts combo gain (Spearman $\\rho = 0.881$, $p = 0.004$; Pearson $r = 0.854$, $p = 0.008$), unifying three findings: (1)~SCB corresponds to $\\text{CIL}(\\text{self}, G) \\ll 0$; (2)~cross-architecture escape succeeds when $\\text{CIL} > 0$; (3)~CIL decays toward zero as generator capability rises, explaining the aggregation ceiling.\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\setlength{\\tabcolsep}{3pt}\n\\caption{Cumulative Information Lift across eight verifier--generator conditions. $\\text{CIL} > 0$: cross-arch NLI on ProofWriter. $\\text{CIL} < 0$: same-model SICA or weak capability gap.}\n\\label{tab:cil_main}\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llrr@{}}\n\\toprule\nGenerator--Verifier & Dataset & CIL & $\\Delta$ (pp) \\\\\n\\midrule\nMistral $\\times$ BART-NLI & PW & $+$1.86 & $+$5.17 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.67 & $+$6.67 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ RoBERTa-NLI & PW & $+$1.42 & $+$4.17 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nQwen-14B $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & PW & $-$0.21 & $+$0.33 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ DeBERTa-NLI & FOLIO & $-$0.47 & $+$2.94 \\\\\n\\addlinespace\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & FOLIO & $-$2.93 & $+$3.43 \\\\\nQwen-14B $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.21 & $-$2.50 \\\\\nMistral $\\times$ SICA & PW & $-$4.61 & $-$2.83 \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Supporting Diagnostics}\n\\label{sec:diagnostics}\n\nFurther diagnostics confirm the mechanism (Appendices~\\ref{app:extraction}--\\ref{app:entropy}). One positive signal emerges: Z3-based semantic deduplication removes ${\\sim}$10\\% additional constraints beyond syntactic dedup and yields a small but significant gain ($p = 0.013$), confirming that the formal channel adds value when redundancy is reduced. The bottleneck is not Z3 but the quality of input constraints: confirmation bias ($\\text{BR} = 4.97\\times$) overwhelms the deduplication gain.\n\n\n\\subsection{Remediation Analysis}\n\\label{sec:remediation}\n\\label{sec:interventions}\n\n\\looseness=-1 Twenty-seven remediation strategies spanning four categories---sampling diversity (temperature, multi-T, diverse prompting), extraction quality (few-shot, iterative refinement, confidence filtering), aggregation (constraint weighting, semantic dedup, MAX-SAT variants), and multi-agent (debate, self-refine, critique)---all produce neutral-to-negative results (Figure~\\ref{fig:interventions-summary}; Tables~\\ref{tab:method_interventions}, \\ref{tab:remediation}, \\ref{tab:additional_interventions}). Of these, 12 represent principled strategies targeting distinct failure modes (independent constraint generation, contrastive extraction, debate-augmented reasoning, etc.), while 15 are parameter variations (temperature, prompt template, few-shot count). At the method level, Debate achieves the lowest $\\kappa = 0.15$ but collapses accuracy by $-18.6$\\,pp ($p < 0.001$); all other methods cluster at $\\kappa \\approx 0.40$--$0.47$, confirming a same-model ceiling (Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:kappa_generalization}). We note an important gap: we do not evaluate trained constraint extractors (e.g., fine-tuned on oracle annotations). Our oracle controls show the extraction-quality ceiling is $+29.90$\\,pp---we consider trained extractors the most promising future direction.\n\n\\begin{figure*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.85\\textwidth]{figures/fig_app_interventions_summary.pdf}\n\\caption{Summary of 27 intervention strategies grouped by four categories. All produce neutral-to-negative accuracy changes relative to Self-Consistency. The grey band indicates $\\pm 1$\\,pp noise zone.}\n\\label{fig:interventions-summary}\n\\end{figure*}",

681	\section{Cross-Model Kappa Analysis}
682	\label{app:cross-model-kappa}
683	
684	Table~\ref{tab:cross_model_kappa} compares trace independence across three model families on the same benchmarks. On FOLIO ($n = 204$), all four models produce $\kappa \geq 0.41$: LLaMA-3.1-8B ($\kappa = 0.41$), Mistral-7B ($\kappa = 0.44$), Qwen2.5-7B ($\kappa = 0.66$), and Qwen2.5-14B ($\kappa = 0.74$). The bottleneck is architecture-independent---no family escapes the correlation floor. Within the Qwen family, $\kappa$ increases from 0.66 (7B) to 0.74 (14B), but Mistral-7B (7B) and LLaMA-3.1-8B (8B) differ by $\Delta\kappa = 0.03$ despite belonging to different families, suggesting that cross-family variation ($\Delta\kappa$ up to 0.22 at similar scale) exceeds within-family scale effects ($\Delta\kappa = 0.08$). Disentangling scale from architecture requires controlled same-family experiments across $\geq$3 scales.
685
